| `agent settings --thinking-budget EFFORT=TOKENS` (`run`-only)                                                               | none (built-in effort→budget ladder)                                                           | Task #36 (pi-parity feature): per-reasoning-effort-level thinking-token-budget override (`agent_core::models::budget_for_effort_with_override`), consulted when `run` derives a thinking budget from `--reasoning-effort` for a `Budget`/`Adaptive`-shape model with no explicit `--thinking`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `mcp_servers` (`settings.json` only — deliberately no CLI flag/env var, `run` and `serve` alike)                            | none (no MCP servers configured)                                                               | MCP client support (`tools/mcp.rs`, rmcp 3.2.0): an array of `{name, transport: "stdio", command, args, env}` or `{name, transport: "http", url, headers}` objects. Each server's `tools/list` becomes `mcp__<name>__<tool>` tools; `resources/list` / `prompts/list` become `mcp__<name>__resource__<n>` / `mcp__<name>__prompt__<n>` tools; argument autocomplete is host-facing via `serve`'s `mcp_complete` (`completion/complete`). Global tier applies unconditionally; project tier is trust-gated (see the Trust Boundaries section above). Servers are configured at startup (hand-edited, like `models.json` — no `agent settings` mutator to add/remove entries mid-process); which configured servers' tools are _advertised_ is session-scoped via `serve`'s `set_mcp_enabled`/`get_mcp` (omit/`null` = all, `[]` = none, allowlist otherwise; resets to all on `new_session`/`switch_session`/`fork`/`clone`). Connect uses `ClientLifecycleMode::Auto` preferring protocol `2026-07-28` (`server/discover`) with legacy initialize fallback; client advertises elicitation/sampling/roots plus the SEP-2663 tasks extension. Tool calls drive `call_tool_once` so SEP-2322 `input_required` rounds and SEP-2663 task handles complete. `serve` answers elicitation (classic nested + MRTR) via `elicitation_request` frames + `elicit` commands (`tests/mcp_elicitation.rs`); headless `run` declines. MCP `notifications/progress` from a `tools/call` bridge into the same `ToolProgress` / `tool_progress` event path bash already uses (`tests/mcp_progress.rs`, `tests/mcp_hotplug.rs`, `tests/mcp_surfaces.rs`). The idle-reap manifest (`mcp_manifest.rs`) caches tools+resources+prompts so a later boot can advertise without spawning. SEP-2663 tasks (`io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks`) are advertised; when `tools/call` returns `resultType: "task"`, the client polls `tasks/get` (honoring `pollIntervalMs`), fulfills in-task `inputRequests` via `tasks/update`, surfaces `statusMessage` as `tool_progress`, and best-effort `tasks/cancel` on abort (`tests/mcp_tasks.rs`: happy path, in-task elicitation, failed/cancelled, abort→`tasks/cancel`) |

`serve`'s `set_thinking`/`cycle_thinking_level` tune the thinking budget at runtime;
`set_auto_compaction`/`set_auto_retry` toggle threshold-triggered compaction / mid-stream retry at
runtime. `--max-tokens` above has no runtime-toggle RPC command of its own — it's fixed for the
process, same as `--temperature`/`--reasoning-effort`.

## Failure Modes

| Failure                                                                                                          | What Actually Happens                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Recovery                                                                                                                                |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Gateway unreachable / non-2xx response                                                                           | `Error::Transport`; `run` exits non-zero printing the error; `serve`'s `prompt` returns `response{success:false}`, process stays up                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Caller retries the command — no built-in retry in this crate                                                                            |
| Tool execution fails (bad path, non-zero exit, regex error)                                                      | `ToolError` → an error `tool_result`, fed back to the model next turn; the run does **not** abort                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Model sees the error text and can adjust its next call                                                                                  |
| `bash` command exceeds `timeout_ms`; `beyond` exceeds 120 s                                                      | `RealRunner` returns `timed_out:true`, killing the whole process group (`GroupKillGuard`); tool turns it into `ToolError::Execution`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Surfaced to the model as a tool error, same as any other failure                                                                        |
| A `prompt` run is cancelled (`abort`, or the process's own shutdown signal) mid tool call                        | `GroupKillGuard`'s drop cascades into killing any backgrounded grandchild process                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `serve` reports `Error::Cancelled` for the turn, not a fault; the session is left at its last committed state                           |
| `serve`'s host `bash` command is cancelled (`abort_bash`/`abort`, or shutdown) mid-stream                        | `Bash::exec` races the runner against the same cancellation token _internally_, not just via an external `Drop` of the whole call — on a trip it flushes whatever the accumulator already captured and returns it as an error result tagged `Command cancelled` (same `text\n\n<status>` convention as a timeout); the raced-away future is still dropped, so `GroupKillGuard` still kills the process group | The `bash` RPC response's `data.result` carries the real partial output, not a bare placeholder — nothing already produced is discarded |
| Model never stops requesting tools                                                                               | Unbounded by default — the run continues until the model ends its turn, cancellation (`abort`/Ctrl-C), or context/token exhaustion stops it. With an opted-in `--max-steps`: `session.steps >= max_steps` → `Error::MaxSteps`; `run` exits non-zero; `serve` returns a failed `response` but keeps serving — resumable with a fresh `prompt`                                                                 | Caller aborts, or issues a fresh `prompt`/`new_session`; opt into `--max-steps` for a hard budget                                       |
| Model refuses the request (`StopReason::Refusal`)                                                                | The run ends immediately without draining queued `steer`/`follow_up`; `serve`'s `prompt` response reports `refused: true`, still `success: true`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Queued steering survives intact for a later `prompt` on the same session                                                                |
| Malformed JSON on `serve`'s stdin                                                                                | Parsed as `Value`, fails → `response{success:false, error:"invalid JSON: …"}`; loop continues                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Client resends a valid command                                                                                                          |
| `serve`'s stdout write fails (broken pipe)                                                                       | Writer task exits its loop; the next `emit!` send observes the closed channel and `break`s the main loop; process exits `Ok(())`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | None by design — a client must reconnect via a new process                                                                              |
| Session file/repo unreadable/corrupt at `serve` startup                                                          | Persistence logs the read/parse failure to stderr, then falls back to a fresh empty `Session` (a missing file stays silent — expected first run); a corrupt line _mid_-file is skipped, not fatal                                                                                                                                                                                                            | None automatic for a truly unreadable file — prior transcript is lost, but the failure is logged                                        |
| Session file write fails after a turn                                                                            | Logged to stderr only (`eprintln!`); the in-memory session and the turn's `response` still report success                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | None automatic — operator must notice the stderr line                                                                                   |
| `edit`'s `old_string` matches 0 or >1 times (no `replace_all`)                                                   | `ToolError::InvalidInput`; the file is **not** modified                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Model adds more surrounding context and retries                                                                                         |
| `grep`/`find` hit `HARD_CAP` (10,000 collected items)                                                            | Walk quits early; output is still sorted+truncated deterministically, but which items entered the set before the cap can vary                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Model narrows the `pattern`/`glob` and retries                                                                                          |
| `grep`/`find` hit an unreadable path (permission denied, a broken symlink, …) and found **no** matches elsewhere | `ToolError::Execution` naming the real path/reason — never a confident "no matches"/"no files matching", matching real `rg`/`fd`'s own non-zero exit the moment they can't read _any_ path in the tree                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Model investigates the named path (e.g. via `bash`) or narrows the search away from it                                                  |
| Same, but real matches **were** found elsewhere in the tree                                                      | The real matches are still returned (a deliberate divergence from pi's own all-or-nothing behavior of discarding every match on any read error) with an `[…incomplete…]` notice naming the reason                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Model treats the result as possibly partial; investigates the named path if it matters                                                  |
| `grep`'s rendered output exceeds `MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES` (50 KiB)                                                     | Truncated at a char boundary with its own marker; wins outright over the match-count marker if both would otherwise fire in the same response                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Model narrows the pattern/path/context and retries                                                                                      |
| `read` on an image that can't fit the 4.5 MB base64 budget even after downscaling                                | `ToolError::InvalidInput` — refused rather than silently sending a payload the provider would reject                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Model resizes the source image itself, or narrows what it asks for                                                                      |
| A working directory is untrusted                                                                                 | `SYSTEM.md`/skill/prompt-template sources from it are silently absent from the system prompt and `get_commands` — not an error (`AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` are unaffected either way — they're never trust-gated); a directory with none of these resources at all is never actually "untrusted" in practice — `has_trust_gated_resources` auto-trusts it                                                       | `agent trust <path>` (persistent) or `--trust-project`/RPC (session)                                                                    |

---

## OAuth / Subscription-Credential Authentication

Full pi parity: logging in with a Claude Pro/Max, GitHub Copilot, or ChatGPT Plus/Pro subscription
instead of a metered API key. See "Provider keys: managed vs. BYO vs. OAuth subscription" above for
_why_ this needed no gateway changes at all and the identity-header tradeoff; this section is _what's
built_ and _where_.

**Protocol flows** (`crate::oauth`, one module per provider — `anthropic`/`github_copilot`/
`openai_codex` — plus shared `pkce`/`device_code`/`callback_server` helpers):

- **Anthropic**: PKCE + a local callback listener on the fixed port `53692`. A real quirk, replicated
  exactly: `state` is the raw PKCE verifier itself, not an independently generated CSRF token. No
  bind-failure fallback — a port conflict is a hard error, matching pi.
- **GitHub Copilot**: device-code (`Iv1.b507a08c87ecfe98`), waits one poll interval before its first
  attempt. `login` first prompts for an optional GitHub Enterprise domain (Task #4 pi-parity fix,
  matching pi's own `loginGitHubCopilot` prompt) — blank defaults to `github.com`; anything else must
  normalize to a real hostname (`github_copilot::normalize_enterprise_domain`, accepting either a bare
  host or a full URL) or the whole login fails outright — and threads the resolved domain through every
  device-flow/token-exchange URL, ending up recorded on the stored credential's own `enterprise_url`
  (previously always `None`, even though `refresh`/`base_url_from_token` already accepted one for a
  credential that had it set some other way). Refreshing re-derives a short-lived Copilot-internal proxy
  token from the long-lived GitHub device-flow token every time; `login` additionally does a
  best-effort, parallel model-"enable" fan-out and records which models are actually available —
  `refresh` re-checks availability but never repeats the enable step.
- **OpenAI Codex**: user picks browser PKCE (port `1455`, an _independent_ random CSRF state, unlike
  Anthropic) or a custom, non-RFC-8628 device-code flow (403/404 both mean "pending"; the token server
  issues its own PKCE verifier for this path, not the client's). No early-refresh buffer, unlike the
  other two providers' 5-minute buffer.

**Storage** (`crate::auth_store::AuthStore`, `~/.claude/auth.json` by default): same shape family as
`SettingsStore`/`TrustStore` — a hand-rolled cross-process `FileLock`, `mutate_locked`'s
re-read-fresh-under-lock discipline, tolerant of a missing/corrupt file. `refreshed()` is the one
new piece of substance: double-checked-locked refresh (the lock is held across the _entire_ refresh,
including the network call, so two concurrent processes sharing a credential can't both consume a
single-use refresh token) that preserves the credential bit-for-bit and records
`last_refresh_error` on failure, rather than deleting it — a transient failure must never cost the
user their login. The file is created at `0600` atomically on first write, and every subsequent write
re-asserts `0600` via an explicit `chmod` afterward — `write_atomic` only ever _preserves_ whatever
permission bits the file already had, so this is what self-heals a mode ever loosened out-of-band
between writes, matching pi's own unconditional `chmodSync` after every `auth-storage.ts` write.

**CLI**: `agent login <provider>` (blocks in-terminal, prints a URL/device-code, races a manual-paste
fallback against the local callback listener), `agent logout <provider>`, `agent auth-status
[provider]` — same dispatch shape as `agent trust`/`agent settings`.

**`serve` RPC**: `login`/`logout`/`auth_status`/`submit_code`/`abort_login` — see the wire-protocol
doc comment at the top of `serve.rs` for the exact frame shapes. `login` runs as a detached background
task (not an inline nested busy-loop like `prompt`'s), pushing `login_progress` frames and an
eventual `response`, precisely because a subscription login has nothing to do with `session`/`agent`
state — every other command keeps working while one is pending; only a second concurrent `login` is
rejected.

**The client-side credential seam**: `gateway_credential::resolve_gateway_credential` is the one
further fallback tier below `--key`/`AI_AGENT_KEY` — consulted only when both are absent, inferring a
provider from the resolved model id (Anthropic: any Claude-dialect id; Codex: a `"codex"`-containing
id; Copilot: _not_ inferred by model prefix at all, since its model set is dynamic — matched only
against a stored credential's own `available_model_ids`) and building an `OAuthCredentialSource` for
it. See `agent_core::client::CredentialSource` above for how that source plugs into `GatewayClient`.

Resolved fresh every time the active model changes, not once for the process's whole life: `run`
(never switches models mid-invocation) calls it exactly once, but `serve` calls it again — via
`serve::build_gateway_client`, which also applies the process's retry policy — at startup (against
whichever model actually ends up active after the anti-bleed session-restore check, not `cfg.model`
directly) and on every `set_model`/`cycle_model`/`switch_session`/`fork`/`clone`/`switch_branch` that
changes it thereafter, swapping the whole `Arc<GatewayClient>` rather than just rebuilding the `Agent`
around the same one. Previously this was resolved exactly once, before `serve` even started, and the
resulting client silently kept serving every later model switch regardless of whether the new model's
provider still matched — a switch from an Anthropic-OAuth model to a GitHub-Copilot- or
OpenAI-Codex-OAuth one sent that stale bearer token/routing to the new provider's endpoint instead of
its own. `set_model`/`cycle_model` fail the whole switch outright if credential resolution for the new
model errors (leaving `current_model`/the persisted lineage untouched, the same "don't fork live state
away from what's durably recorded" precedent `record_model_change` already sets); the four
session-restoration commands (`switch_session`/`fork`/`clone`/`switch_branch`) instead warn to stderr
and keep the previous client, matching their existing best-effort "restore whatever was recorded"
posture — the next `prompt` then surfaces its own clear transport-level error if that client genuinely
can't serve the restored model.

## MCP Server OAuth

A wholly separate OAuth mechanism from the section above — that one authenticates _this agent_ to a
_model_ provider (Anthropic/Copilot/Codex); this one authenticates it to a _configured MCP server_
(`tools/mcp.rs`'s `mcp_servers`, `http` transport only — `stdio` servers have no login of their own,
see that module's doc comment). Different protocol too: not each provider's own bespoke flow, but the
MCP spec's own standardized OAuth 2.1 (SEP-985 protected-resource-metadata discovery, RFC 7591 dynamic
client registration, PKCE) — implemented almost entirely by `rmcp`'s own `transport::auth` module
(`AuthorizationManager`/`AuthorizationSession`), not hand-rolled the way `crate::oauth`'s three
provider flows are (MCP servers don't each have their own bespoke client id/endpoint quirks the way
Anthropic/Copilot/Codex do — the whole point of the spec's own discovery + DCR is that a generic client
never needs provider-specific code).

**Storage** (`mcp_auth_store.rs::McpAuthStore`, `~/.claude/mcp_auth.json` by default): same shape family
as `AuthStore`/`SettingsStore`/`TrustStore` (own small `FileLock`, atomic writes,
re-read-fresh-under-lock) but keyed by MCP server _name_ (`mcp_servers`' own `name` field) rather than
a fixed provider enum — a `ScopedMcpCredentialStore` implements `rmcp`'s own `CredentialStore` trait
scoped to one server's slot in the shared file, which is what an `AuthorizationManager` actually reads
and writes through; `rmcp` itself calls `save()` after both the initial token exchange and every later
refresh, so persistence is a side effect of driving the manager, not something this crate's own code
does explicitly.

**CLI**: `agent mcp-login <name>` (blocks in-terminal, prints the authorization URL, waits on a local
callback) — `name` must already be a configured `http`-transport `mcp_servers` entry. Unlike `agent
login`'s three providers, each pre-registered against one _fixed_ callback port, `mcp-login` picks a
fresh local port every run and registers _that_ via dynamic client registration — there's no
pre-registered redirect URI to reuse, since MCP servers don't know about this client in advance.
Reuses `oauth::callback_server::CallbackServer` (the identical local-listener primitive `agent login`'s
own PKCE flows use) for the actual wait; the CSRF `state` value needed to validate the callback is
extracted directly from the authorization URL `AuthorizationSession` generates (it doesn't otherwise
expose the raw token) rather than threaded through as a separate return value. `agent mcp-logout <name>`
removes the stored credential; idempotent, matching `agent logout`.

**`tools::mcp::connect_http`'s consumption side**: before connecting, checks
`McpAuthStore::has_credential` for the server's name — a plain file-existence check, so the common case
(no MCP OAuth login at all) costs nothing beyond that. When one exists, restores it
(`AuthorizationManager::initialize_from_store`) and calls `get_access_token()` (refreshing
transparently — `rmcp`'s own 30-second-early buffer means most real-world token lifetimes trigger a
refresh well before actual expiry) to get a current bearer token, attached to the streamable-HTTP
transport's `auth_header`. A missing/dead credential is never itself treated as fatal here — the
connect attempt proceeds unauthenticated (correct for the common case of a server that needs no auth
at all) and _that_ attempt's own failure (if the server does require it) is enriched with a hint to run
`agent mcp-login <name>`, rather than this function pre-emptively deciding a server needs auth.

Tested end to end in `tests/mcp_oauth.rs` against a real, hand-rolled OAuth-protected MCP fixture
(metadata discovery, dynamic client registration, a real authorize-then-redirect exchange, a token
endpoint supporting both `authorization_code` and `refresh_token` grants, and _actual_ bearer-token
enforcement on the MCP endpoint itself) — the full login flow, a subsequent tool call authenticated by
the resulting token, transparent refresh, `mcp-logout`, and the clear-failure-naming-`mcp-login` path
for a server nobody has ever logged into.
